The issue reports that the login code input field only allows numeric keyboard input, but the auth code contains both letters and numbers. Users have to manually type the `hackerspub://` URL as a workaround.

Let me find the relevant code.

> TOOL

Search for:\n1. The login screen or auth code input composable/activity\n2. Any keyboard type configuration (KeyboardType, inputType, etc.)\n3. Files related to authentication flow\n\nBe thorough - check for KeyboardType.Number, KeyboardType.Decimal, inputType=\"number\", etc."

```
Perfect! I have all the information needed. Let me compile my findings:

## Summary

I found the keyboard type issue in your Android app's authentication flow. Here's what I discovered:

### Issue Location
**File:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/screens/auth/SignInScreen.kt`

### The Problem
In the `VerificationStep` composable (lines 202-298), the verification code input field is configured with:

```kotlin
keyboardOptions = KeyboardOptions(
    keyboardType = KeyboardType.Number,  // LINE 253 - THIS IS THE PROBLEM
    imeAction = ImeAction.Done
)
```

**Line 253** sets `keyboardType = KeyboardType.Number`, which restricts the keyboard to numeric input only. However, the verification code being sent to users apparently contains both **letters and numbers**, making it impossible for users to enter alphanumeric codes.

### Related Information
- **ViewModel:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/screens/auth/SignInViewModel.kt` - Handles code verification logic
- **Username field (line 164-167):** Correctly uses `KeyboardType.Text` - no issue here
- **String resources:** The verification description states "Enter the verification code sent to your email" but doesn't specify the format

### The Fix
Change line 253 from:
```kotlin
keyboardType = KeyboardType.Number,
```

To:
```kotlin
keyboardType = KeyboardType.Ascii,  // or KeyboardType.Text
```

This would allow users to enter alphanumeric verification codes. `KeyboardType.Ascii` is ideal for codes, or `KeyboardType.Text` if more special characters might be needed.

title:	Push notification support
state:	OPEN
author:	malkoG
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	67
--
## Context

The app targets both **F-Droid** and **Google Play Store**, which constrains notification strategy.

## Alternatives considered

| Approach | Verdict | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| **UnifiedPush + embedded FCM** | Rejected | F-Droid users must install a separate distributor app (ntfy, Gotify, etc.) — poor UX. Backend also lacks WebPush (RFC8291/RFC8292) support. |
| **FCM only** | Rejected | Proprietary, banned by F-Droid inclusion policy. |
| **SSE / WebSocket** | Rejected | Needs backend support, battery concerns. |
| **WorkManager polling** | **Chosen** | No extra apps, no backend changes, works everywhere. |

## Chosen approach: WorkManager polling

- Use a `PeriodicWorkRequest` (every 15 min, the minimum Android allows)
- Call the existing `Notifications` GraphQL query in the background
- Compare against last-seen notification ID to detect new items
- Show Android system notifications for new items
- Wire up the unread notification dot in bottom nav (existing TODO in `HackersPubApp.kt`)

## Tasks

- [ ] Create a `NotificationWorker` extending `CoroutineWorker`
- [ ] Register periodic work in `Application.onCreate()`
- [ ] Persist last-seen notification ID (DataStore)
- [ ] Build and show Android system notifications
- [ ] Request `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission (Android 13+)
- [ ] Wire up unread notification dot on bottom nav tab

## Future enhancement

If the Hackers' Pub backend adds WebPush support, UnifiedPush + embedded FCM distributor can be layered on top for real-time delivery. The polling would remain as a fallback.
```

## Project Architecture Analysis: Hackers Pub Android

I've completed a thorough exploration of the Hackers Pub Android project. Here's a comprehensive guide for implementing a WorkManager-based notification polling feature:

### 1. **PROJECT STRUCTURE**

│   │   │   │   ├── remote/       # Would be for direct API calls

### 3. **GRAPHQL LAYER**

**Notifications Query Structure:**
```graphql
query Notifications($after: String) {
    viewer {
        id
        notifications(first: 20, after: $after) {
            edges { cursor, node { ... } }
            pageInfo { hasNextPage, endCursor }
        }
    }
}
```

**Key Points:**
- Located: `/app/src/main/graphql/pub/hackers/android/operations.graphql`
- Apollo Client 4.1.0 with normalized cache (SQLite)
- Automatic code generation to `pub.hackers.android.graphql` package
- Uses fragments: `ActorFields`, `PostFields`, `SharedPostFields`
- Supports pagination with cursor-based navigation
- GraphQL endpoint: `https://hackers.pub/graphql`

**Notification Types in domain model:**
```kotlin

    Follow, Mention, Reply, Quote, Share, React
}
```

**Repository method:**
```kotlin

```

### 4. **APPLICATION CLASS**

**File:** `/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/HackersPubApplication.kt`

```kotlin

class HackersPubApplication : Application()
```

**Current setup:**
- Uses Hilt for dependency injection (no manual setup needed)

### 5. **AUTH/TOKEN STORAGE**

**SessionManager** (`/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/local/SessionManager.kt`)

Uses **DataStore Preferences** for secure token storage:
```kotlin
@Singleton
class SessionManager @Inject constructor(
    private val dataStore: DataStore<Preferences>

```

**Stored keys:**
- `SESSION_TOKEN_KEY` - Bearer token for API calls
- `USER_ID_KEY`, `USERNAME_KEY`, `USER_HANDLE_KEY`, `USER_NAME_KEY`, `USER_AVATAR_KEY`

**Critical for WorkManager:**
```kotlin
val sessionToken: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    preferences[SESSION_TOKEN_KEY]
}
val isLoggedIn: Flow<Boolean> // Derived from session token
```

**Background access pattern:**
- WorkManager's `CoroutineWorker` can suspend collect flows
- Already configured in `AppModule.provideApolloClient()` which reads token:
```kotlin

```

### 8. **DEPENDENCY INJECTION SETUP**

**Framework:** Hilt (com.google.dagger.hilt)

**DI Module:** `/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/di/AppModule.kt`

```kotlin
@Module
@InstallIn(SingletonComponent::class)
object AppModule {
    @Provides @Singleton
    fun provideDataStore(...): DataStore<Preferences>
    
    @Provides @Singleton
    fun provideSessionManager(dataStore): SessionManager
    
    @Provides @Singleton
    fun provideApolloClient(context, sessionManager): ApolloClient
}
```

**Key patterns:**
- All singletons in `SingletonComponent`
- OkHttp auth interceptor injects token into all Apollo requests
- Apollo configured with normalized SQLite cache
- SessionManager injected everywhere needed

**For WorkManager:**
- Create a `@HiltWorker` for the notification polling worker
- Inject dependencies directly into worker constructor
- Register with Hilt's automatic binding

### 9. **DATASTORE USAGE PATTERNS**

**Two DataStore instances:**

1. **Session DataStore** (SessionManager)
   - Name: `"hackerspub_prefs"`
   - Stores: auth tokens, user info
   - Singleton injection point

2. **Preferences DataStore** (PreferencesManager)
   - Name: `"app_preferences"`
   - Stores: UI settings, search history, font size, etc.
   - Used for app-level settings

**PreferencesManager** (`/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/local/PreferencesManager.kt`):
- Manages UI preferences (confirm before delete, timeline length, font size, in-app browser)
- Can be extended for notification polling settings

**Pattern to follow:**
```kotlin
val myPreference: Flow<T> = context.dataStore.data.map { prefs ->
    prefs[MY_KEY] ?: defaultValue
}

suspend fun setMyPreference(value: T) {
    context.dataStore.edit { prefs ->
        prefs[MY_KEY] = value
    }
}
```

### 10. **AUTHENTICATION FLOW**

**Location:** `/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/screens/auth/SignInScreen.kt`

**Process:**
1. User enters username → `loginByUsername()` mutation
2. Receives login challenge token
3. Redirected to browser verification (deep link)
4. Deep link returns to app with token + code
5. `completeLoginChallenge()` mutation returns session
6. Session saved to DataStore via `SessionManager.saveSession()`
7. Apollo client's interceptor automatically adds Bearer token

**Key for background work:**
- Token persisted in SessionManager
- WorkManager can access it via injected SessionManager
- Token refresh: Would need to check if server supports refresh tokens (currently not implemented)

> AGENT

Good — I have a solid understanding of the codebase. The architecture is well-suited for this feature:

- **GraphQL `Notifications` query** already exists with pagination
- **Hilt DI** is set up with `SingletonComponent`
- **DataStore** is already used for session tokens and preferences
- **Bottom nav** already has a `hasNotificationDot` property with a TODO to wire it up

The issue spec is quite detailed and prescriptive. I have one clarifying question before proposing the design:

**Should the notification polling only run when the user is logged in?** I assume yes — the worker should check `SessionManager.isLoggedIn` and skip if there's no session token. But I want to confirm, and also ask: when the user logs out, should we cancel the periodic work entirely, or just let it no-op?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

Got it — cancel the periodic work on logout.

Now let me propose approaches for a key design decision: **how to propagate "has unread" state to the bottom nav**.

### Approach A: SharedFlow from a singleton `NotificationStateManager`

A `@Singleton` class holds a `StateFlow<Boolean>` for `hasUnread`. The worker updates it via DataStore, the UI collects it. Simple, decoupled.

### Approach B: Direct DataStore observation in `AppViewModel`

`AppViewModel` directly observes a `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID` key from DataStore. The worker writes new IDs there; the ViewModel compares against the current value to derive `hasUnread`.

### Approach C: Broadcast via WorkManager's `Result.success(outputData)`

Worker outputs data, observed via `WorkManager.getWorkInfosForUniqueWorkLiveData()`. No extra class needed, but awkward to observe from Compose and doesn't survive worker replacement.

**My recommendation: Approach A.** It cleanly separates concerns — the worker writes to DataStore, the singleton reads it and exposes a flow, and the UI just collects. It's testable and doesn't couple the UI to WorkManager internals.

Does that work for you, or do you prefer a different approach?

> DEVELOPER

Go with approach A

> AGENT

Good. Let me present the design in sections.

### Section 1: Dependencies & Permissions

**New dependencies** in `libs.versions.toml`:
- `androidx.work:work-runtime-ktx:2.10.0`
- `androidx.hilt:hilt-work:1.2.0` (for `@HiltWorker` support)
- `androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler:1.2.0` (KSP processor)

**Manifest additions**:
- `<uses-permission android:name="android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS" />` (Android 13+)
- No `INTERNET` needed — already present

**No new modules** — everything lives in the `app` module following existing patterns.

Does this look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

### Section 2: Data Layer

**New DataStore keys** in `PreferencesManager`:
- `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID: String` — the ID of the most recent notification the user has seen (updated when they open the notifications screen)
- `LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID: String` — the ID of the newest notification from the last poll (updated by the worker)

**`NotificationStateManager`** (`data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt`):
- `@Singleton`, injected with `DataStore<Preferences>`
- Exposes `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>` — derived by comparing the two IDs above
- `suspend fun updateLastPolledId(id: String)` — called by worker
- `suspend fun markAsSeen()` — called when user opens notifications screen (sets last-seen = last-polled)
- `suspend fun clear()` — called on logout

**Why two IDs instead of a boolean flag?** A boolean `hasUnread` in DataStore would get stale — if the worker sets `true` and the app restarts, we'd need to re-check anyway. Two IDs let us derive the state accurately after any restart.

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

### Section 3: NotificationWorker

**`NotificationWorker`** (`data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt`):
- `@HiltWorker` extending `CoroutineWorker`
- Injected with: `HackersPubRepository`, `SessionManager`, `NotificationStateManager`

**Logic**:
1. Check `sessionManager.isLoggedIn.first()` — if false, return `Result.success()` (no-op)
2. Call `repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)` to fetch the latest page
3. If the first notification ID differs from the last polled ID:
   - Call `notificationStateManager.updateLastPolledId(newId)`
   - Build and show Android system notification(s) for the new items
4. Return `Result.success()` on success, `Result.retry()` on network failure

**System notifications**:
- Create a notification channel `"hackerspub_notifications"` with name "Hackers' Pub"
- Group multiple new notifications under a summary
- Each notification has a `PendingIntent` that deep-links into the app's notifications screen
- Notification content: actor name + action (e.g. "alice mentioned you", "bob followed you")

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

### Section 4: Worker Registration & Lifecycle

**`HackersPubApplication.onCreate()`**:
- Initialize Hilt's `HiltWorkerFactory` via `Configuration.Provider`
- No eager work scheduling here — that's tied to login state

**`SessionManager` or login flow**:
- After successful login: enqueue `PeriodicWorkRequestBuilder<NotificationWorker>(15, TimeUnit.MINUTES)` with unique name `"notification_poll"`, `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.KEEP`
- Constraints: `NetworkType.CONNECTED` (skip if offline)

**On logout**:
- `WorkManager.cancelUniqueWork("notification_poll")`
- `NotificationStateManager.clear()`

**On app launch (already logged in)**:
- `AppViewModel.init` checks `isLoggedIn` and enqueues the periodic work if not already running (using `KEEP` policy so it doesn't restart the interval)

**Why not schedule in Application.onCreate()?** We'd have to check login state there, and it's cleaner to tie it to the auth lifecycle. `KEEP` policy makes it safe to call enqueue multiple times.

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

### Section 5: UI Wiring

**`AppViewModel`**:
- Inject `NotificationStateManager`
- Expose `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>` from it
- Call `ensureNotificationPolling()` in `init` block (enqueues work if logged in)

**`HackersPubApp.kt`**:
- Collect `appViewModel.hasUnread` as state
- Pass it to the notifications `BottomNavItem`:
  ```kotlin
  hasNotificationDot = hasUnread
  ```

**`NotificationsViewModel`**:
- Inject `NotificationStateManager`
- Call `notificationStateManager.markAsSeen()` when notifications load successfully (clears the dot)

**POST_NOTIFICATIONS permission**:
- Request in `MainActivity` on Android 13+ using `rememberLauncherForActivityResult`
- Prompt after first successful login, not on app launch (better UX)
- Worker still polls regardless — it just can't show system notifications without the permission

That covers the full design. Does this all look good? If so, I'll write the spec document.

> DEVELOPER

Yes

  "content": "# Notification Polling via WorkManager\n\n**Issue:** [#67 — Push notification support](https://github.com/hackers-pub/android/issues/67)\n**Date:** 2026-03-25\n\n## Overview\n\nBackground notification polling using WorkManager's `PeriodicWorkRequest` (15-minute interval). Polls the existing GraphQL `Notifications` query, shows Android system notifications for new items, and wires up the unread dot on the bottom nav.\n\n## Dependencies & Permissions\n\n### New dependencies (`libs.versions.toml`)\n\n- `androidx.work:work-runtime-ktx:2.10.0`\n- `androidx.hilt:hilt-work:1.2.0`\n- `androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler:1.2.0` (KSP processor)\n\n### Manifest\n\n- `<uses-permission android:name=\"android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS\" />`\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐     writes      ┌──────────────────────┐    derives     ┌──────────────┐\n│ NotificationWorker│ ──────────────→ │ NotificationStateManager│ ──────────→ │ hasUnread Flow│\n│ (CoroutineWorker) │                │ (@Singleton)            │              └──────┬───────┘\n└────────┬─────────┘                └───────────┬────────────┘                     │\n         │                                      │                                  │ collect\n         │ fetches                              │ markAsSeen()                     ▼\n         ▼                                      │                          ┌──────────────┐\n┌─────────────────┐                   ┌─────────┴──────────┐              │ AppViewModel  │\n│ HackersPubRepo   │                   │NotificationsViewModel│              │ → BottomNav   │\n│ .getNotifications│                   └────────────────────┘              └──────────────┘\n└─────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Data Layer\n\n### DataStore Keys (in `PreferencesManager`)\n\n| Key | Type | Purpose |\n|-----|------|---------|\n| `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID` | `String` | ID of the newest notification the user has seen (updated when notifications screen opens) |\n| `LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID` | `String` | ID of the newest notification from the last background poll |\n\nTwo IDs instead of a boolean flag so the unread state can be accurately derived after app restart without re-fetching.\n\n### `NotificationStateManager` (`data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt`)\n\n- `@Singleton`, injected with `DataStore<Preferences>`\n- `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>` — true when last-polled ID differs from last-seen ID\n- `suspend fun updateLastPolledId(id: String)` — called by worker after fetching new notifications\n- `suspend fun markAsSeen()` — called when user opens notifications screen; sets last-seen = last-polled\n- `suspend fun clear()` — called on logout; resets both IDs\n\n## NotificationWorker (`data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt`)\n\n`@HiltWorker` extending `CoroutineWorker`. Injected with `HackersPubRepository`, `SessionManager`, `NotificationStateManager`.\n\n### Execution flow\n\n1. Check `sessionManager.isLoggedIn.first()` — return `Result.success()` if not logged in\n2. Call `repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)` to fetch the latest page\n3. Compare first notification ID against last polled ID\n4. If different:\n   - `notificationStateManager.updateLastPolledId(newId)`\n   - Build and post Android system notifications for the new items\n5. Return `Result.success()` on success, `Result.retry()` on network failure\n\n### System notifications\n\n- Channel ID: `\"hackerspub_notifications\"`, name: \"Hackers' Pub\"\n- Group multiple notifications under a summary\n- Each notification includes: actor name + action (e.g. \"alice mentioned you\")\n- `PendingIntent` deep-links to the notifications screen\n- Respects `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission — worker polls regardless, but only posts notifications if permission is granted\n\n## Worker Registration & Lifecycle\n\n### Scheduling\n\n- **After login:** Enqueue `PeriodicWorkRequestBuilder<NotificationWorker>(15, TimeUnit.MINUTES)` with unique name `\"notification_poll\"`, policy `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.KEEP`, constraint `NetworkType.CONNECTED`\n- **On app launch (already logged in):** `AppViewModel.init` enqueues with `KEEP` policy (safe to call multiple times; does not restart the interval)\n\n### Cancellation\n\n- **On logout:** `WorkManager.cancelUniqueWork(\"notification_poll\")` + `NotificationStateManager.clear()`\n\n### WorkManager initialization\n\n- `HackersPubApplication` implements `Configuration.Provider` to provide Hilt's `HiltWorkerFactory`\n\n## UI Wiring\n\n### `AppViewModel`\n\n- Inject `NotificationStateManager`\n- Expose `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>`\n- Call `ensureNotificationPolling()` in `init` (enqueues work if logged in)\n\n### `HackersPubApp.kt`\n\n- Collect `appViewModel.hasUnread`\n- Pass to notifications `BottomNavItem`: `hasNotificationDot = hasUnread`\n\n### `NotificationsViewModel`\n\n- Inject `NotificationStateManager`\n- Call `markAsSeen()` when notifications load successfully\n\n### POST_NOTIFICATIONS permission\n\n- Request in `MainActivity` on Android 13+ via `rememberLauncherForActivityResult`\n- Prompt after first successful login, not on cold start\n- Worker polls regardless of permission; it just skips posting system notifications if denied\n\n## New Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt` | Singleton managing unread state via DataStore |\n| `data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt` | Background polling worker |\n\n## Modified Files\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `gradle/libs.versions.toml` | Add WorkManager + Hilt Work dependencies |\n| `app/build.gradle.kts` | Add new dependency entries |\n| `AndroidManifest.xml` | Add `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission |\n| `HackersPubApplication.kt` | Implement `Configuration.Provider` for HiltWorkerFactory |\n| `di/AppModule.kt` | Provide `NotificationStateManager`, `WorkManager` |\n| `data/local/PreferencesManager.kt` | Add two new DataStore keys |\n| `ui/AppViewModel.kt` | Expose `hasUnread`, enqueue polling on init |\n| `ui/HackersPubApp.kt` | Wire `hasNotificationDot` from `hasUnread` state |\n| `ui/screens/notifications/NotificationsViewModel.kt` | Call `markAsSeen()` on load |\n| `MainActivity.kt` | Request `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission after login |\n"
}

---

**VERDICT: ISSUES_FOUND**

Here is my detailed review of the design spec at `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-25-notification-polling-design.md`.

---

## What the spec does well

The spec is well-structured and covers the core architecture cleanly. The choice of WorkManager with `PeriodicWorkRequest` is appropriate for background polling on Android. The dual-ID approach for unread state (last-seen vs. last-polled) is a good design that avoids re-fetching on app restart. The architecture diagram, file inventory, and lifecycle management (login/logout/app-launch) are all clearly laid out. The existing TODO comment at line 152 of `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/ui/HackersPubApp.kt` (`// TODO: wire up unread notification state`) confirms this feature was anticipated.

---

## Critical Issues (must fix before implementation)

### 1. DataStore conflict: spec places keys in the wrong DataStore

The spec says to add `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID` and `LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID` to `PreferencesManager`, and the new `NotificationStateManager` is injected with `DataStore<Preferences>`. However, the codebase has **two separate DataStore instances**:

- `SessionManager` uses the `DataStore<Preferences>` provided by `AppModule` (backed by `"hackerspub_prefs"`, defined in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/di/AppModule.kt` line 23).
- `PreferencesManager` creates its **own** DataStore via `Context.preferencesDataStore` (backed by `"app_preferences"`, defined in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/local/PreferencesManager.kt` line 17) and injects `@ApplicationContext Context` rather than `DataStore<Preferences>`.

The spec says `NotificationStateManager` is "injected with `DataStore<Preferences>`." If this is the Hilt-provided instance from `AppModule`, it would be the `"hackerspub_prefs"` store, not the `"app_preferences"` store that `PreferencesManager` uses. The spec also says keys go "in `PreferencesManager`," creating an inconsistency.

**Recommendation:** The spec should explicitly state which DataStore instance `NotificationStateManager` uses. Since `NotificationStateManager` is a standalone `@Singleton` managing its own concern, the cleanest option is to inject the same `DataStore<Preferences>` from `AppModule` (the `"hackerspub_prefs"` store where session data already lives, since notification state is session-scoped). The keys should be owned by `NotificationStateManager` itself, not added to `PreferencesManager`. Update the "Modified Files" table to remove `PreferencesManager` and instead note that the keys live inside `NotificationStateManager`.

### 2. `AppModule` says to "Provide `NotificationStateManager`, `WorkManager`" but `NotificationStateManager` is already `@Singleton` with `@Inject constructor`

The spec's Modified Files table says `di/AppModule.kt` should "Provide `NotificationStateManager`, `WorkManager`". But looking at the spec's own description, `NotificationStateManager` is annotated `@Singleton` with constructor injection (matching the project's pattern for `SessionManager`). If that is the case, it does not need a `@Provides` method in `AppModule` -- Hilt will pick it up automatically. However, `WorkManager` **does** need a `@Provides` method (or use `WorkManager.getInstance(context)`).

**Recommendation:** Clarify in the spec that `AppModule` only needs a `@Provides` for `WorkManager.getInstance(context)`. `NotificationStateManager` should use constructor injection with `@Singleton @Inject constructor(...)`, consistent with `SessionManager`.

### 3. Missing: disable default WorkManager initialization in the manifest

The spec says `HackersPubApplication` implements `Configuration.Provider` for `HiltWorkerFactory`, but it does not mention disabling the default `WorkManagerInitializer`. Without this, the app will crash or log errors because WorkManager initializes twice. On AndroidX WorkManager 2.6+, you must add to `AndroidManifest.xml`:

```xml
<provider
    android:name="androidx.startup.InitializationProvider"
    android:authorities="${applicationId}.androidx-startup"
    android:exported="false"
    tools:node="merge">
    <meta-data
        android:name="androidx.work.WorkManagerInitializer"
        tools:node="remove" />
</provider>
```

**Recommendation:** Add a manifest entry to the "Modified Files" section specifying the removal of the default initializer, or alternatively note that the app should use `Application.getWorkManagerConfiguration()` override pattern. This is a must for custom WorkManager initialization.

### 4. Missing: logout must cancel worker AND clear system notifications

The spec says logout calls `WorkManager.cancelUniqueWork(...)` and `NotificationStateManager.clear()`, but it does not mention canceling posted system notifications. After logout, any existing notifications in the system tray from the previous session should be dismissed.

**Recommendation:** Add `NotificationManager.cancelAll()` to the logout flow, and specify which component is responsible for calling it (likely the `SettingsViewModel.signOut()` method).

---

## Important Issues (should fix)

### 5. `hasUnread` derived as `StateFlow<Boolean>` but the underlying DataStore emits cold `Flow`

The spec says `NotificationStateManager.hasUnread` is a `StateFlow<Boolean>`. However, DataStore's `.data` property returns a cold `Flow`. Converting it to a `StateFlow` requires a coroutine scope (e.g., `stateIn(scope, ...)`). But `NotificationStateManager` is a plain `@Singleton`, not a ViewModel, so it has no built-in scope.

**Recommendation:** Either (a) specify that `NotificationStateManager` takes a `CoroutineScope` (application-scoped, provided by Hilt) to convert the flow via `stateIn`, or (b) change the type to `Flow<Boolean>` instead of `StateFlow<Boolean>` and let the consumers (AppViewModel) do the `stateIn` conversion in their own `viewModelScope`. Option (b) is simpler and more consistent with how `SessionManager.isLoggedIn` is exposed as a plain `Flow<Boolean>`.

### 6. No notification channel creation step specified

The spec mentions channel ID `"hackerspub_notifications"` and name "Hackers' Pub" but does not specify where or when the `NotificationChannel` is created. On Android 8+ (API 26, which is this app's minSdk), notification channels must be created before posting notifications.

**Recommendation:** Specify that channel creation happens in `HackersPubApplication.onCreate()` or in the worker itself before posting the first notification. The former is more idiomatic and is the standard pattern.

### 7. Spec references `repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)` but worker runs outside of any Activity/ViewModel lifecycle

The `HackersPubRepository` is injected with `ApolloClient`, which in turn uses an `OkHttpClient` with an auth interceptor that calls `runBlocking { sessionManager.sessionToken.first() }` (line 50 of `AppModule.kt`). This will work from the worker context, but it is worth noting explicitly in the spec that the auth token will be read synchronously during the network call. If the token has expired or been revoked, the GraphQL query will fail, and the spec's `Result.retry()` on network failure would retry indefinitely against an invalid token.

**Recommendation:** Add a note that the worker should distinguish between network failures (retry-worthy) and auth failures (not retry-worthy, should return `Result.success()` and possibly clear state). Consider checking the GraphQL response for auth errors specifically.

### 8. Race condition between "markAsSeen" and concurrent worker polling

If the worker fires while the user is on the notifications screen, the sequence could be: (1) user opens screen, (2) worker writes a new `lastPolledId`, (3) `markAsSeen()` sets `lastSeenId = lastPolledId`. This is actually fine. But consider the reverse: (1) worker fetches new notifications, (2) user opens the screen and calls `markAsSeen()` using the *old* `lastPolledId`, (3) worker writes the new `lastPolledId`. Now `hasUnread` flips back to `true` even though the user just saw the screen.

**Recommendation:** Add a note that `markAsSeen()` should snapshot the current `lastPolledId` at the time it is called (which it does by design, since "sets last-seen = last-polled"), and that this minor race is acceptable because the user can pull-to-refresh. Alternatively, `NotificationsViewModel` could call `markAsSeen()` after each successful refresh, not just on initial load, which the spec's "Call `markAsSeen()` when notifications load successfully" already implies. Just confirm this means on every load/refresh, not only the first time.

### 9. ProGuard / R8 rules for WorkManager and Hilt workers

The app has `isMinifyEnabled = true` in the release build. The existing proguard rules at `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/proguard-rules.pro` do not include rules for WorkManager or Hilt workers. The `@HiltWorker` annotation and `CoroutineWorker` may need keep rules to avoid stripping.

**Recommendation:** Add a note in the spec to verify that the `hilt-work` library's consumer proguard rules are sufficient, or add explicit keep rules for `NotificationWorker` in `proguard-rules.pro`. Typically `hilt-work` bundles its own rules, but this should be verified during implementation.

---

## Suggestions (nice to have)

### 10. Consider exposing a "notification count" instead of just a boolean dot

The `BottomNavItem` currently only has `hasNotificationDot: Boolean`. For a richer UX in the future, the spec could note that tracking the count of new notifications (not just whether there are any) would enable a badge number. This is not required now but would inform the DataStore schema -- storing the last-seen ID makes it hard to derive a count without re-fetching.

### 11. The spec does not mention backoff strategy for `Result.retry()`

WorkManager's default exponential backoff starts at 10 seconds. The spec should note whether the default is acceptable or if a custom backoff policy should be set on the `PeriodicWorkRequest`.

### 12. Deep link from system notification to notifications screen

The spec says the `PendingIntent` deep-links to the notifications screen. But the current navigation setup uses Compose Navigation routes, not Android deep links registered in the manifest. The spec should clarify the mechanism -- likely constructing an `Intent` that targets `MainActivity` with extras or a URI that the app's nav controller can interpret. This requires some implementation detail to avoid it being ambiguous.

### 13. Spec says "Add two new DataStore keys" under PreferencesManager in Modified Files but also says keys live in NotificationStateManager

This is related to issue #1 above. The "Modified Files" table row for `data/local/PreferencesManager.kt` should be removed if the keys are owned by `NotificationStateManager`.

---

## Summary of actionable items

| # | Severity | Item |
|---|----------|------|
| 1 | Critical | Clarify which DataStore instance `NotificationStateManager` uses; remove `PreferencesManager` from the modified files list |
| 2 | Critical | Clarify that `NotificationStateManager` uses constructor injection, not a `@Provides` method; only `WorkManager` needs `@Provides` |
| 3 | Critical | Add manifest entry to disable default `WorkManagerInitializer` |
| 4 | Critical | Add `NotificationManager.cancelAll()` on logout |
| 5 | Important | Change `hasUnread` from `StateFlow<Boolean>` to `Flow<Boolean>`, or specify the coroutine scope for `stateIn` |
| 6 | Important | Specify where `NotificationChannel` is created |
| 7 | Important | Distinguish auth errors from network errors in the worker retry logic |
| 8 | Important | Confirm `markAsSeen()` is called on every refresh, not just initial load |
| 9 | Important | Note proguard verification needed for `@HiltWorker` + minification |
| 10 | Suggestion | Consider notification count instead of boolean-only unread state |
| 11 | Suggestion | Specify backoff policy for `Result.retry()` |
| 12 | Suggestion | Clarify PendingIntent deep-link mechanism for Compose Navigation |
| 13 | Suggestion | Remove `PreferencesManager` row from Modified Files table (duplicate of #1) |

    52→- `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>` — true when last-polled ID differs from last-seen ID
    53→- `suspend fun updateLastPolledId(id: String)` — called by worker after fetching new notifications
    54→- `suspend fun markAsSeen()` — called when user opens notifications screen; sets last-seen = last-polled

    57→## NotificationWorker (`data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt`)
    58→
    59→`@HiltWorker` extending `CoroutineWorker`. Injected with `HackersPubRepository`, `SessionManager`, `NotificationStateManager`.

    61→### Execution flow
    62→
    63→1. Check `sessionManager.isLoggedIn.first()` — return `Result.success()` if not logged in
    64→2. Call `repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)` to fetch the latest page

    66→4. If different:
    67→   - `notificationStateManager.updateLastPolledId(newId)`
    68→   - Build and post Android system notifications for the new items
    69→5. Return `Result.success()` on success, `Result.retry()` on network failure

    72→
    73→- Channel ID: `"hackerspub_notifications"`, name: "Hackers' Pub"

    82→
    83→- **After login:** Enqueue `PeriodicWorkRequestBuilder<NotificationWorker>(15, TimeUnit.MINUTES)` with unique name `"notification_poll"`, policy `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.KEEP`, constraint `NetworkType.CONNECTED`

    85→

    87→
    88→- **On logout:** `WorkManager.cancelUniqueWork("notification_poll")` + `NotificationStateManager.clear()`
    89→
    90→### WorkManager initialization
    91→
    92→- `HackersPubApplication` implements `Configuration.Provider` to provide Hilt's `HiltWorkerFactory`

    95→

  "content": "# Notification Polling via WorkManager\n\n**Issue:** [#67 — Push notification support](https://github.com/hackers-pub/android/issues/67)\n**Date:** 2026-03-25\n\n## Overview\n\nBackground notification polling using WorkManager's `PeriodicWorkRequest` (15-minute interval). Polls the existing GraphQL `Notifications` query, shows Android system notifications for new items, and wires up the unread dot on the bottom nav.\n\n## Dependencies & Permissions\n\n### New dependencies (`libs.versions.toml`)\n\n- `androidx.work:work-runtime-ktx:2.10.0`\n- `androidx.hilt:hilt-work:1.2.0`\n- `androidx.hilt:hilt-compiler:1.2.0` (KSP processor)\n\n### Manifest\n\n- `<uses-permission android:name=\"android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS\" />`\n- Disable default `WorkManagerInitializer` to allow custom Hilt-based initialization:\n  ```xml\n  <provider\n      android:name=\"androidx.startup.InitializationProvider\"\n      android:authorities=\"${applicationId}.androidx-startup\"\n      android:exported=\"false\"\n      tools:node=\"merge\">\n      <meta-data\n          android:name=\"androidx.work.WorkManagerInitializer\"\n          tools:node=\"remove\" />\n  </provider>\n  ```\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐     writes      ┌──────────────────────┐    derives     ┌──────────────┐\n│ NotificationWorker│ ──────────────→ │ NotificationStateManager│ ──────────→ │ hasUnread Flow│\n│ (CoroutineWorker) │                │ (@Singleton)            │              └──────┬───────┘\n└────────┬─────────┘                └───────────┬────────────┘                     │\n         │                                      │                                  │ collect\n         │ fetches                              │ markAsSeen()                     ▼\n         ▼                                      │                          ┌──────────────┐\n┌─────────────────┐                   ┌─────────┴──────────┐              │ AppViewModel  │\n│ HackersPubRepo   │                   │NotificationsViewModel│              │ → BottomNav   │\n│ .getNotifications│                   └────────────────────┘              └──────────────┘\n└─────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Data Layer\n\n### `NotificationStateManager` (`data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt`)\n\n`@Singleton` with `@Inject constructor`, injected with the Hilt-provided `DataStore<Preferences>` (the `\"hackerspub_prefs\"` instance from `AppModule`). Notification state is session-scoped, so it belongs alongside session data.\n\n**DataStore keys** (owned by this class, not `PreferencesManager`):\n\n| Key | Type | Purpose |\n|-----|------|---------|\n| `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID` | `String` | ID of the newest notification the user has seen (updated on every notifications screen load/refresh) |\n| `LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID` | `String` | ID of the newest notification from the last background poll |\n\nTwo IDs instead of a boolean flag so the unread state can be accurately derived after app restart without re-fetching.\n\n**API:**\n\n- `val hasUnread: Flow<Boolean>` — cold Flow derived from DataStore, true when last-polled ID differs from last-seen ID. Consumers (e.g. `AppViewModel`) convert to `StateFlow` via `stateIn` in their own scope.\n- `suspend fun updateLastPolledId(id: String)` — called by worker after fetching new notifications\n- `suspend fun markAsSeen()` — called on every successful notifications load/refresh; sets last-seen = last-polled\n- `suspend fun clear()` — called on logout; resets both IDs\n\n## NotificationWorker (`data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt`)\n\n`@HiltWorker` extending `CoroutineWorker`. Injected with `HackersPubRepository`, `SessionManager`, `NotificationStateManager`.\n\n### Execution flow\n\n1. Check `sessionManager.isLoggedIn.first()` — return `Result.success()` if not logged in\n2. Call `repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)` to fetch the latest page\n3. On failure: distinguish auth errors (return `Result.success()`, not worth retrying) from network errors (return `Result.retry()` with default exponential backoff starting at 10s)\n4. Compare first notification ID against last polled ID\n5. If different:\n   - `notificationStateManager.updateLastPolledId(newId)`\n   - Build and post Android system notifications for the new items (only if `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission is granted)\n6. Return `Result.success()`\n\n### System notifications\n\n- Notification channel created in `HackersPubApplication.onCreate()`: ID `\"hackerspub_notifications\"`, name \"Hackers' Pub\"\n- Group multiple notifications under a summary\n- Each notification includes: actor name + action (e.g. \"alice mentioned you\")\n- `PendingIntent` targets `MainActivity` with an extra/URI that the Compose nav controller routes to the notifications screen\n- Respects `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission — worker polls regardless, but only posts notifications if permission is granted\n\n## Worker Registration & Lifecycle\n\n### Scheduling\n\n- **After login:** Enqueue `PeriodicWorkRequestBuilder<NotificationWorker>(15, TimeUnit.MINUTES)` with unique name `\"notification_poll\"`, policy `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.KEEP`, constraint `NetworkType.CONNECTED`\n- **On app launch (already logged in):** `AppViewModel.init` enqueues with `KEEP` policy (safe to call multiple times; does not restart the interval)\n\n### Cancellation\n\n- **On logout:**\n  - `WorkManager.cancelUniqueWork(\"notification_poll\")`\n  - `NotificationStateManager.clear()`\n  - `NotificationManager.cancelAll()` — dismiss any posted system notifications from the previous session\n\n### WorkManager initialization\n\n- `HackersPubApplication` implements `Configuration.Provider` to provide Hilt's `HiltWorkerFactory`\n- Default `WorkManagerInitializer` disabled in manifest (see Manifest section above)\n\n## UI Wiring\n\n### `AppViewModel`\n\n- Inject `NotificationStateManager` and `WorkManager`\n- Expose `val hasUnread: StateFlow<Boolean>` via `notificationStateManager.hasUnread.stateIn(viewModelScope, SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5000), false)`\n- Call `ensureNotificationPolling()` in `init` (enqueues work if logged in)\n\n### `HackersPubApp.kt`\n\n- Collect `appViewModel.hasUnread`\n- Pass to notifications `BottomNavItem`: `hasNotificationDot = hasUnread`\n\n### `NotificationsViewModel`\n\n- Inject `NotificationStateManager`\n- Call `markAsSeen()` on every successful load and refresh (not just initial load)\n\n### POST_NOTIFICATIONS permission\n\n- Request in `MainActivity` on Android 13+ via `rememberLauncherForActivityResult`\n- Prompt after first successful login, not on cold start\n- Worker polls regardless of permission; it just skips posting system notifications if denied\n\n## New Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt` | Singleton managing unread state via DataStore |\n| `data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt` | Background polling worker |\n\n## Modified Files\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `gradle/libs.versions.toml` | Add WorkManager + Hilt Work dependencies |\n| `app/build.gradle.kts` | Add new dependency entries |\n| `AndroidManifest.xml` | Add `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission, disable default `WorkManagerInitializer` |\n| `HackersPubApplication.kt` | Implement `Configuration.Provider` for HiltWorkerFactory, create notification channel |\n| `di/AppModule.kt` | Add `@Provides` for `WorkManager.getInstance(context)` |\n| `ui/AppViewModel.kt` | Expose `hasUnread`, enqueue polling on init |\n| `ui/HackersPubApp.kt` | Wire `hasNotificationDot` from `hasUnread` state |\n| `ui/screens/notifications/NotificationsViewModel.kt` | Call `markAsSeen()` on every load/refresh |\n| `MainActivity.kt` | Request `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission after login |\n\n## Notes\n\n- **ProGuard/R8:** Verify that `hilt-work`'s bundled consumer rules are sufficient for `@HiltWorker` with `isMinifyEnabled = true`. Add explicit keep rules for `NotificationWorker` if needed.\n- **Race condition:** If the worker fires while the user is on the notifications screen, `markAsSeen()` is called on every refresh, so the dot will clear on the next pull-to-refresh. This minor race is acceptable.\n- **Future:** If the backend adds WebPush support, UnifiedPush + embedded FCM can be layered on top for real-time delivery. The polling remains as a fallback.\n"
}

Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase and the design spec. Let me verify one more thing -- the `SessionManager` uses constructor injection (not `@Provides`), which is relevant for understanding the DI pattern consistency.

Let me now systematically verify each of the 9 previously-raised issues against the updated spec.

---

## Review Verdict: **APPROVED**

All nine issues from the previous review pass have been addressed in the updated spec. Here is the point-by-point verification:

### Issue 1: DataStore keys should be owned by NotificationStateManager, not PreferencesManager
**FIXED.** Line 56 explicitly states: "**DataStore keys** (owned by this class, not `PreferencesManager`)". The keys `LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID` and `LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID` are scoped to `NotificationStateManager`. This is consistent with the existing codebase where `PreferencesManager` (in `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/local/PreferencesManager.kt`) owns its own keys and uses a separate DataStore (`"app_preferences"`), while `SessionManager` uses the shared `"hackerspub_prefs"` DataStore. The spec correctly places notification state in the session-scoped DataStore (line 54).

### Issue 2: NotificationStateManager uses constructor injection, only WorkManager needs @Provides
**FIXED.** Line 52-53 specifies `@Singleton` with `@Inject constructor`, injected with the Hilt-provided `DataStore<Preferences>`. The Modified Files table (line 153) correctly limits `AppModule` changes to adding only a `@Provides` for `WorkManager.getInstance(context)`. This is consistent with how `SessionManager` already works in the codebase -- it uses `@Inject constructor` but has an explicit `@Provides` in `AppModule` (line 37-39 of `AppModule.kt`). One note: `SessionManager` currently has a redundant `@Provides` despite having `@Inject constructor`; the spec avoids repeating that pattern for `NotificationStateManager`, which is correct.

### Issue 3: Must disable default WorkManagerInitializer in manifest
**FIXED.** Lines 21-32 provide the exact XML snippet for disabling the default `WorkManagerInitializer` via the `androidx-startup` provider with `tools:node="remove"`. Line 111 confirms `HackersPubApplication` implements `Configuration.Provider` to provide Hilt's `HiltWorkerFactory`. Line 112 cross-references back to the manifest section.

### Issue 4: Logout must cancel system notifications via NotificationManager.cancelAll()
**FIXED.** Line 107 explicitly includes `NotificationManager.cancelAll()` in the logout sequence, with the comment "dismiss any posted system notifications from the previous session". The full logout sequence (lines 104-107) is: cancel the work, clear state, dismiss notifications.

### Issue 5: hasUnread should be Flow<Boolean> not StateFlow<Boolean>
**FIXED.** Line 67 declares `val hasUnread: Flow<Boolean>` as a cold Flow derived from DataStore. Line 67 also explains: "Consumers (e.g. `AppViewModel`) convert to `StateFlow` via `stateIn` in their own scope." Line 119 in the AppViewModel section shows the exact `stateIn` call with `SharingStarted.WhileSubscribed(5000)`. This is the correct layered approach -- the state manager exposes a cold Flow, and each consumer scopes it to their own lifecycle.

### Issue 6: Specify where NotificationChannel is created
**FIXED.** Line 89 explicitly states: "Notification channel created in `HackersPubApplication.onCreate()`". Line 152 in the Modified Files table confirms `HackersPubApplication.kt` will be modified to create the notification channel. This is the standard Android pattern since the channel must exist before any notification is posted, and `Application.onCreate()` is the earliest reliable point.

### Issue 7: Distinguish auth errors from network errors in worker retry
**FIXED.** Line 80 spells out the error-handling strategy: "distinguish auth errors (return `Result.success()`, not worth retrying) from network errors (return `Result.retry()` with default exponential backoff starting at 10s)". This prevents the worker from pointlessly retrying when the token is invalid/expired while still recovering from transient network failures.

### Issue 8: Confirm markAsSeen() on every refresh
**FIXED.** Line 69 states `markAsSeen()` is "called on every successful notifications load/refresh". Line 156 in the Modified Files table confirms: "Call `markAsSeen()` on every load/refresh". Line 162 even addresses the race condition where the worker fires while the user is on the notifications screen, noting that `markAsSeen()` on every refresh handles this gracefully.

### Issue 9: ProGuard note needed
**FIXED.** Lines 161 provides the ProGuard/R8 guidance: "Verify that `hilt-work`'s bundled consumer rules are sufficient for `@HiltWorker` with `isMinifyEnabled = true`. Add explicit keep rules for `NotificationWorker` if needed." This is appropriate given that `isMinifyEnabled = true` in the release build type (line 38 of `app/build.gradle.kts`).

### Additional observations (no action required)

The spec is well-structured and internally consistent. A few things I noted positively:

- The architecture diagram (lines 36-47) clearly shows data flow direction.
- The two-ID approach (last-seen vs. last-polled, lines 62-63) is a sound design that avoids re-fetching on restart.
- The `KEEP` policy for `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy` (line 99-100) is correct -- it prevents restarting the interval on every app launch.
- The new files table (lines 140-143) and modified files table (lines 146-158) provide a clear implementation roadmap.
- The future note about WebPush/UnifiedPush (line 163) shows forward-thinking design without over-engineering the current solution.

**Relevant file:** `/Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-25-notification-polling-design.md`

     4→import androidx.datastore.core.DataStore
     5→import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.Preferences
     6→import androidx.datastore.preferences.preferencesDataStore
     7→import com.apollographql.apollo.ApolloClient
     8→import com.apollographql.apollo.cache.normalized.normalizedCache
     9→import com.apollographql.apollo.cache.normalized.sql.SqlNormalizedCacheFactory
    10→import com.apollographql.apollo.network.okHttpClient
    11→import dagger.Module
    12→import dagger.Provides
    13→import dagger.hilt.InstallIn
    14→import dagger.hilt.android.qualifiers.ApplicationContext
    15→import dagger.hilt.components.SingletonComponent
    16→import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.first
    17→import kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking
    18→import okhttp3.Interceptor
    19→import okhttp3.OkHttpClient
    20→import pub.hackers.android.data.local.SessionManager
    21→import javax.inject.Singleton
    22→
    23→private val Context.dataStore: DataStore<Preferences> by preferencesDataStore(name = "hackerspub_prefs")
    24→
    25→@Module
    26→@InstallIn(SingletonComponent::class)
    27→object AppModule {
    28→
    29→    @Provides
    30→    @Singleton
    31→    fun provideDataStore(@ApplicationContext context: Context): DataStore<Preferences> {
    32→        return context.dataStore
    33→    }
    34→
    35→    @Provides
    36→    @Singleton
    37→    fun provideSessionManager(dataStore: DataStore<Preferences>): SessionManager {
    38→        return SessionManager(dataStore)
    39→    }
    40→
    41→    @Provides
    42→    @Singleton
    43→    fun provideApolloClient(
    44→        @ApplicationContext context: Context,
    45→        sessionManager: SessionManager
    46→    ): ApolloClient {
    47→        val sqlNormalizedCacheFactory = SqlNormalizedCacheFactory(context, "apollo_cache.db")
    48→
    49→        val authInterceptor = Interceptor { chain ->
    50→            val token = runBlocking { sessionManager.sessionToken.first() }
    51→            val request = if (token != null) {
    52→                chain.request().newBuilder()
    53→                    .addHeader("Authorization", "Bearer $token")
    54→                    .build()
    55→            } else {
    56→                chain.request()
    57→            }
    58→            chain.proceed(request)
    59→        }
    60→
    61→        val okHttpClient = OkHttpClient.Builder()
    62→            .addInterceptor(authInterceptor)
    63→            .build()
    64→
    65→        return ApolloClient.Builder()
    66→            .serverUrl("https://hackers.pub/graphql")
    67→            .okHttpClient(okHttpClient)
    68→            .normalizedCache(sqlNormalizedCacheFactory)
    69→            .build()
    70→    }
    71→}
    72→

     1→package pub.hackers.android.ui.screens.notifications
     2→
     3→import androidx.lifecycle.ViewModel
     4→import androidx.lifecycle.viewModelScope
     5→import dagger.hilt.android.lifecycle.HiltViewModel
     6→import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.MutableStateFlow
     7→import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.StateFlow
     8→import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.asStateFlow
     9→import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.update
    10→import kotlinx.coroutines.launch
    11→import pub.hackers.android.data.repository.HackersPubRepository
    12→import pub.hackers.android.domain.model.Notification
    13→import java.time.Instant
    14→import javax.inject.Inject
    15→
    16→data class NotificationsUiState(
    17→    val notifications: List<Notification> = emptyList(),
    18→    val isLoading: Boolean = false,
    19→    val isRefreshing: Boolean = false,
    20→    val isLoadingMore: Boolean = false,
    21→    val hasNextPage: Boolean = false,
    22→    val endCursor: String? = null,
    23→    val error: String? = null
    24→)
    25→
    26→@HiltViewModel
    27→class NotificationsViewModel @Inject constructor(
    28→    private val repository: HackersPubRepository
    29→) : ViewModel() {
    30→
    31→    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(NotificationsUiState())
    32→    val uiState: StateFlow<NotificationsUiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()
    33→
    34→    private var lastLoadTime: Instant? = null
    35→    private val staleThresholdSeconds = 60L
    36→
    37→    init {
    38→        loadNotifications()
    39→    }
    40→
    41→    fun refreshIfStale() {
    42→        val last = lastLoadTime ?: return
    43→        if (Instant.now().epochSecond - last.epochSecond > staleThresholdSeconds) {
    44→            refresh()
    45→        }
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    private fun loadNotifications() {
    49→        viewModelScope.launch {
    50→            _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoading = true, error = null) }
    51→
    52→            repository.getNotifications()
    53→                .onSuccess { result ->
    54→                    lastLoadTime = Instant.now()
    55→                    _uiState.update {
    56→                        it.copy(
    57→                            notifications = result.notifications,
    58→                            hasNextPage = result.hasNextPage,
    59→                            endCursor = result.endCursor,
    60→                            isLoading = false
    61→                        )
    62→                    }
    63→                }
    64→                .onFailure { error ->
    65→                    _uiState.update {
    66→                        it.copy(
    67→                            error = error.message,
    68→                            isLoading = false
    69→                        )
    70→                    }
    71→                }
    72→        }
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    fun refresh() {
    76→        viewModelScope.launch {
    77→            _uiState.update { it.copy(isRefreshing = true, error = null) }
    78→
    79→            repository.getNotifications(refresh = true)
    80→                .onSuccess { result ->
    81→                    lastLoadTime = Instant.now()
    82→                    _uiState.update {
    83→                        it.copy(
    84→                            notifications = result.notifications,
    85→                            hasNextPage = result.hasNextPage,
    86→                            endCursor = result.endCursor,
    87→                            isRefreshing = false
    88→                        )
    89→                    }
    90→                }
    91→                .onFailure { error ->
    92→                    _uiState.update {
    93→                        it.copy(
    94→                            error = error.message,
    95→                            isRefreshing = false
    96→                        )
    97→                    }
    98→                }
    99→        }
   100→    }
   101→
   102→    fun loadMore() {
   103→        val currentState = _uiState.value
   104→        if (!currentState.hasNextPage || currentState.isLoadingMore) return
   105→
   106→        viewModelScope.launch {
   107→            _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoadingMore = true) }
   108→
   109→            repository.getNotifications(after = currentState.endCursor)
   110→                .onSuccess { result ->
   111→                    _uiState.update {
   112→                        it.copy(
   113→                            notifications = it.notifications + result.notifications,
   114→                            hasNextPage = result.hasNextPage,
   115→                            endCursor = result.endCursor,
   116→                            isLoadingMore = false
   117→                        )
   118→                    }
   119→                }
   120→                .onFailure {
   121→                    _uiState.update { it.copy(isLoadingMore = false) }
   122→                }
   123→        }
   124→    }
   125→}
   126→

    19→data class DeepLinkData(

    21→    val code: String
    22→)
    23→
    24→@AndroidEntryPoint
    25→class MainActivity : ComponentActivity() {
    26→
    27→    private var deepLinkData by mutableStateOf<DeepLinkData?>(null)
    28→
    29→    override fun onCreate(savedInstanceState: Bundle?) {
    30→        super.onCreate(savedInstanceState)
    31→        handleDeepLink(intent)
    32→        enableEdgeToEdge()
    33→        setContent {
    34→            HackersPubTheme {
    35→                Surface(
    36→                    modifier = Modifier.fillMaxSize(),
    37→                    color = LocalAppColors.current.background
    38→                ) {
    39→                    HackersPubApp(deepLinkData = deepLinkData)
    40→                }
    41→            }
    42→        }
    43→    }
    44→
    45→    override fun onNewIntent(intent: Intent) {
    46→        super.onNewIntent(intent)
    47→        handleDeepLink(intent)
    48→    }
    49→
    50→    private fun handleDeepLink(intent: Intent?) {
    51→        val data = intent?.data ?: return
    52→        if (data.scheme == "hackerspub" && data.host == "verify") {
    53→            val token = data.getQueryParameter("token")
    54→            val code = data.getQueryParameter("code")
    55→            if (token != null && code != null) {
    56→                deepLinkData = DeepLinkData(token = token, code = code)
    57→            }
    58→        }
    59→    }
    60→}

     1→package pub.hackers.android.data.local

     3→import androidx.datastore.core.DataStore
     4→import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.Preferences
     5→import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.edit

     9→import javax.inject.Inject
    10→import javax.inject.Singleton
    11→
    12→@Singleton
    13→class SessionManager @Inject constructor(
    14→    private val dataStore: DataStore<Preferences>
    15→) {
    16→    companion object {
    17→        private val SESSION_TOKEN_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("session_token")
    18→        private val USERNAME_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("username")
    19→        private val USER_ID_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("user_id")
    20→        private val USER_HANDLE_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("user_handle")
    21→        private val USER_NAME_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("user_name")
    22→        private val USER_AVATAR_KEY = stringPreferencesKey("user_avatar")
    23→    }
    24→
    25→    val sessionToken: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    26→        preferences[SESSION_TOKEN_KEY]
    27→    }
    28→
    29→    val isLoggedIn: Flow<Boolean> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    30→        preferences[SESSION_TOKEN_KEY] != null
    31→    }
    32→
    33→    val username: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    34→        preferences[USERNAME_KEY]
    35→    }
    36→
    37→    val userId: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    38→        preferences[USER_ID_KEY]
    39→    }
    40→
    41→    val userHandle: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    42→        preferences[USER_HANDLE_KEY]
    43→    }
    44→
    45→    val userName: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    46→        preferences[USER_NAME_KEY]
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    val userAvatar: Flow<String?> = dataStore.data.map { preferences ->
    50→        preferences[USER_AVATAR_KEY]
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    suspend fun saveSession(
    54→        token: String,
    55→        userId: String,
    56→        username: String,
    57→        handle: String,
    58→        name: String,
    59→        avatarUrl: String
    60→    ) {
    61→        dataStore.edit { preferences ->
    62→            preferences[SESSION_TOKEN_KEY] = token
    63→            preferences[USER_ID_KEY] = userId
    64→            preferences[USERNAME_KEY] = username
    65→            preferences[USER_HANDLE_KEY] = handle
    66→            preferences[USER_NAME_KEY] = name
    67→            preferences[USER_AVATAR_KEY] = avatarUrl
    68→        }
    69→    }
    70→
    71→    suspend fun clearSession() {
    72→        dataStore.edit { preferences ->
    73→            preferences.remove(SESSION_TOKEN_KEY)
    74→            preferences.remove(USER_ID_KEY)
    75→            preferences.remove(USERNAME_KEY)
    76→            preferences.remove(USER_HANDLE_KEY)
    77→            preferences.remove(USER_NAME_KEY)
    78→            preferences.remove(USER_AVATAR_KEY)
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→}

    21→data class SettingsUiState(
    22→    val userName: String? = null,
    23→    val userHandle: String? = null,
    24→    val userAvatar: String? = null,
    25→    val appVersion: String = "",
    26→    val cacheSize: String = "",
    27→    val isSignedOut: Boolean = false,
    28→    val message: String? = null,
    29→    val confirmBeforeDelete: Boolean = true,
    30→    val confirmBeforeShare: Boolean = false,
    31→    val timelineMaxLength: Int = 0,
    32→    val useInAppBrowser: Boolean = true,
    33→    val fontSizePercent: Int = 100
    34→)
    35→
    36→@HiltViewModel
    37→class SettingsViewModel @Inject constructor(
    38→    private val sessionManager: SessionManager,
    39→    private val preferencesManager: PreferencesManager,
    40→    private val repository: HackersPubRepository,
    41→    private val apolloClient: ApolloClient,
    42→    @ApplicationContext private val context: Context
    43→) : ViewModel() {
    44→
    45→    private val _uiState = MutableStateFlow(SettingsUiState())
    46→    val uiState: StateFlow<SettingsUiState> = _uiState.asStateFlow()
    47→
    48→    init {
    49→        loadUserInfo()
    50→        loadAppVersion()
    51→        loadPreferences()
    52→        calculateCacheSize()
    53→    }
    54→
    55→    private fun loadUserInfo() {
    56→        viewModelScope.launch {
    57→            val name = sessionManager.userName.first()
    58→            val handle = sessionManager.userHandle.first()
    59→            val avatar = sessionManager.userAvatar.first()
    60→
    61→            _uiState.update {
    62→                it.copy(
    63→                    userName = name,
    64→                    userHandle = handle,
    65→                    userAvatar = avatar
    66→                )
    67→            }
    68→        }
    69→    }
    70→
    71→    private fun loadAppVersion() {
    72→        try {
    73→            val packageInfo = context.packageManager.getPackageInfo(context.packageName, 0)
    74→            val version = packageInfo.versionName ?: "Unknown"
    75→            val versionCode = packageInfo.longVersionCode
    76→            _uiState.update { it.copy(appVersion = "$version ($versionCode)") }
    77→        } catch (_: Exception) {
    78→            _uiState.update { it.copy(appVersion = "Unknown") }
    79→        }
    80→    }
    81→
    82→    private fun loadPreferences() {
    83→        viewModelScope.launch {
    84→            val confirmDelete = preferencesManager.confirmBeforeDelete.first()
    85→            val confirmShare = preferencesManager.confirmBeforeShare.first()
    86→            val maxLength = preferencesManager.timelineMaxLength.first()
    87→            val inAppBrowser = preferencesManager.useInAppBrowser.first()
    88→            val fontSize = preferencesManager.fontSizePercent.first()
    89→
    90→            _uiState.update {
    91→                it.copy(
    92→                    confirmBeforeDelete = confirmDelete,
    93→                    confirmBeforeShare = confirmShare,
    94→                    timelineMaxLength = maxLength,
    95→                    useInAppBrowser = inAppBrowser,
    96→                    fontSizePercent = fontSize
    97→                )
    98→            }
    99→        }
   100→    }
   101→
   102→    fun setConfirmBeforeDelete(value: Boolean) {
   103→        _uiState.update { it.copy(confirmBeforeDelete = value) }
   104→        viewModelScope.launch { preferencesManager.setConfirmBeforeDelete(value) }
   105→    }
   106→
   107→    fun setConfirmBeforeShare(value: Boolean) {
   108→        _uiState.update { it.copy(confirmBeforeShare = value) }
   109→        viewModelScope.launch { preferencesManager.setConfirmBeforeShare(value) }
   110→    }
   111→
   112→    fun setTimelineMaxLength(value: Int) {
   113→        _uiState.update { it.copy(timelineMaxLength = value) }
   114→        viewModelScope.launch { preferencesManager.setTimelineMaxLength(value) }
   115→    }
   116→
   117→    fun setUseInAppBrowser(value: Boolean) {
   118→        _uiState.update { it.copy(useInAppBrowser = value) }
   119→        viewModelScope.launch { preferencesManager.setUseInAppBrowser(value) }
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    fun setFontSizePercent(value: Int) {
   123→        _uiState.update { it.copy(fontSizePercent = value) }
   124→        viewModelScope.launch { preferencesManager.setFontSizePercent(value) }
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    fun signOut() {
   128→        viewModelScope.launch {
   129→            val sessionId = sessionManager.sessionToken.first()
   130→            if (sessionId != null) {
   131→                repository.revokeSession(sessionId)
   132→            }
   133→            sessionManager.clearSession()
   134→            apolloClient.apolloStore.clearAll()
   135→            _uiState.update { it.copy(isSignedOut = true) }
   136→        }
   137→    }
   138→
   139→    private fun calculateCacheSize() {
   140→        viewModelScope.launch {
   141→            val cacheDir = context.cacheDir
   142→            val size = getDirSize(cacheDir)
   143→            _uiState.update { it.copy(cacheSize = formatBytes(size)) }
   144→        }
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    private fun getDirSize(dir: java.io.File): Long {
   148→        var size = 0L
   149→        if (dir.isDirectory) {
   150→            dir.listFiles()?.forEach { file ->
   151→                size += if (file.isDirectory) getDirSize(file) else file.length()
   152→            }
   153→        }
   154→        return size
   155→    }
   156→
   157→    private fun formatBytes(bytes: Long): String {
   158→        return when {
   159→            bytes < 1024 -> "$bytes B"
   160→            bytes < 1024 * 1024 -> String.format("%.1f KB", bytes / 1024.0)
   161→            bytes < 1024 * 1024 * 1024 -> String.format("%.1f MB", bytes / (1024.0 * 1024))
   162→            else -> String.format("%.1f GB", bytes / (1024.0 * 1024 * 1024))
   163→        }
   164→    }
   165→
   166→    fun clearCache() {
   167→        viewModelScope.launch {
   168→            try {
   169→                apolloClient.apolloStore.clearAll()
   170→                // Also clear file cache
   171→                context.cacheDir.listFiles()?.forEach { it.deleteRecursively() }
   172→                calculateCacheSize()
   173→                _uiState.update { it.copy(message = "Cache cleared") }
   174→            } catch (e: Exception) {
   175→                _uiState.update { it.copy(message = "Failed to clear cache") }
   176→            }
   177→        }
   178→    }
   179→
   180→    fun clearMessage() {
   181→        _uiState.update { it.copy(message = null) }
   182→    }
   183→}
   184→

  "content": "# Notification Polling Implementation Plan\n\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.\n\n**Goal:** Add background notification polling via WorkManager so users receive Android system notifications and see an unread dot on the bottom nav.\n\n**Architecture:** A `CoroutineWorker` polls the existing GraphQL `Notifications` query every 15 minutes. A singleton `NotificationStateManager` persists last-seen/last-polled IDs in DataStore and exposes a `Flow<Boolean>` for unread state. The UI collects this flow to drive the bottom nav dot.\n\n**Tech Stack:** WorkManager 2.10, Hilt Work 1.2, DataStore Preferences, Apollo GraphQL, Jetpack Compose\n\n**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-25-notification-polling-design.md`\n\n---\n\n## File Structure\n\n### New files\n| File | Responsibility |\n|------|---------------|\n| `app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/worker/NotificationWorker.kt` | Background polling worker — fetches notifications, updates state, posts system notifications |\n\n### Modified files\n| File NotificationStateManager\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `app/src/main/java/pub/hackers/android/data/local/NotificationStateManager.kt`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create the file**\n\n```kotlin\npackage pub.hackers.android.data.local\n\nimport androidx.datastore.core.DataStore\nimport androidx.datastore.preferences.core.Preferences\nimport androidx.datastore.preferences.core.edit\nimport androidx.datastore.preferences.core.stringPreferencesKey\nimport kotlinx.coroutines.flow.Flow\nimport kotlinx.coroutines.flow.map\nimport javax.inject.Inject\nimport javax.inject.Singleton\n\n@Singleton\nclass NotificationStateManager @Inject constructor(\n    private val dataStore: DataStore<Preferences>\n) {\n    companion object {\n        private val LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID = stringPreferencesKey(\"last_seen_notification_id\")\n        private val LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID = stringPreferencesKey(\"last_polled_notification_id\")\n    }\n\n    val hasUnread: Flow<Boolean> = dataStore.data.map { prefs ->\n        val lastSeen = prefs[LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID]\n        val lastPolled = prefs[LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID]\n        lastPolled != null && lastPolled != lastSeen\n    }\n\n    suspend fun updateLastPolledId(id: String) {\n        dataStore.edit { prefs ->\n            prefs[LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID] = id\n        }\n    }\n\n    suspend fun markAsSeen() {\n        dataStore.edit { prefs ->\n            val lastPolled = prefs[LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID]\n            if (lastPolled != null) {\n                prefs[LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID] = lastPolled\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    suspend fun clear() {\n        dataStore.edit { prefs ->\n            prefs.remove(LAST_SEEN_NOTIFICATION_ID)\n            prefs.remove(LAST_POLLED_NOTIFICATION_ID)\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Verify it compiles**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/kodingwarrior/fediverse/hackerspub-android && ./gradlew
